China a dingmath@mail.nankai.edu.cn, b sunhui@nankai.edu.cn Abstract. The study of truncated theta series has regained vitality since Andrews and Merca’s work on the truncated version of Euler’s pentagonal number theorem in 2012. Moreover, Andrews and Merca, Guo and Zeng independently conjectured that the truncated Jacobi triple product series has nonnegative coefficients, which has been proved analytically by Mao and combinatorially by Yee. In 2021, Merca proposed a stronger version of the conjecture for the truncated Jacobi triple product series, which is for positive integers 1 ≤ S < R 1\leq S<R with k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , the coefficient of q n q^{n} in the theta series ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = k ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q R ​ j ​ ( j + 1 ) / 2 ​ ( q − S ​ j − q ( j + 1 ) ​ S ) ( q S , q R − S , q R ) ∞ \frac{(-1)^{k}\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{Rj(j+1)/2}\left(q^{-Sj}-q^{(j+1)S}\right)}{\left(q^{S},q^{R-S};q^{R}\right)_{\infty}} is nonnegative. Recently, some very special cases of this conjecture have been proved and studied. In this paper, for any given R , S R,S and k k , we provide a systematic method to determine the lower bound N N of n n such that when n ≥ N n\geq N , Merca’s stronger conjecture holds. More precisely, we first treat the infinite q q -shift factorial in the denominator of the above theta series as the product of the following two parts 1 ( q s , q r − s , q r ) 2 ⋅ 1 ( q 2 ​ r + s , q 3 ​ r − s , q r ) ∞ := ∑ n = 0 ∞ p 4 ​ ( n ) ​ q n ⋅ ∑ n = 0 ∞ g s , r ​ ( n ) ​ q n , \frac{1}{\left(q^{s},q^{r-s};q^{r}\right)_{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(q^{2r+s},q^{3r-s};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p_{4}(n)q^{n}\cdot\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}g_{s,r}(n)q^{n}, where s = S / ( S , R ) , r = R / ( S , R ) s=S/(S,R),r=R/(S,R) are coprime. The first part can be interpreted in terms of partition functions p 4 ​ ( n ) p_{4}(n) which are the number of partitions of n n only with parts s , r + s , r − s , 2 ​ r − s s,r+s,r-s,2r-s . The second part can be seen as a nonmodular infinite product. For p 4 ​ ( n ) p_{4}(n) , we obtained the general upper and lower bounds by using the residue theorem and the properties for partition functions. Further multiplied by the numerator of the theta series, we derive a lower bound of n n which satisfies that the coefficients of the subsequent terms are all nonnegative. For g s , r ​ ( n ) g_{s,r}(n) , we obtain the lower and upper bounds by applying the circle method on nonmodular infinite products, from which we also deduce an asymptotic formula for g s , r ​ ( n ) g_{s,r}(n) . Then, by considering the convolution of these two parts, we confirm Merca’s stronger conjecture when n ≥ N n\geq N . Consequently, we show that when k k is sufficiently large, it only needs to deal with the first part to provide the bound N N and Merca’s stronger conjecture holds directly for any S S and R R . Keywords: truncated theta series; partition function; Jacobi triple product series; nonmodular infinite product; circle method AMS Classification: 05A17, 33D15, 11P55, 11P82 1 Introduction In 2012, Andrews and Merca investigated the truncated form of Euler’s pentagonal number theorem and obtained the following result [ 3 , Lemma 1.2] : ( − 1 ) k − 1 ( q , q ) ∞ ​ ∑ j = − k k − 1 ( − 1 ) j ​ q j ⁡ ( 3 ​ j + 1 ) / 2 = ( − 1 ) k − 1 + ∑ n = 1 ∞ q ( k 2 ) + ( k + 1 ) ​ n ( q , q ) n ​ [ n − 1 k − 1 ] . \displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{(q;q)_{\infty}}\sum_{j=-k}^{k-1}(-1)^{j}q^{j(3j+1)/2}=(-1)^{k-1}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{q^{\binom{k}{2}+(k+1)n}}{(q;q)_{n}}{n-1\brack k-1}. (1.1) One can see that the coefficient of q n q^{n} on the right hand side of ( 1.1 ) is nonnegative, for which Andrews and Merca also gave a corresponding partition interpretation, which also leads to that ( − 1 ) k − 1 ​ ∑ j = 0 k − 1 ( − 1 ) j ​ ( p ⁡ ( n − j ⁡ ( 3 ​ j + 1 ) / 2 ) − p ⁡ ( n − j ⁡ ( 3 ​ j + 5 ) / 2 − 1 ) ) ≥ 0 . \displaystyle(-1)^{k-1}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(-1)^{j}\big(p(n-j(3j+1)/2)-p(n-j(3j+5)/2-1)\big)\geq 0. Soon after, Guo and Zeng studied the truncated forms of Gauss’ triangular and square numbers series in [ 20 ] , from which they also derive the recurrence relations for partition functions. Since then the truncated forms for theta series have attracted more and more attentions. In 2018, by applying the Rogers-Fine identity [ 37 , p.15] , Andrews and Merca [ 4 ] obtained the unified truncated forms for the above three classical theta series, which are further generalized by Xia, Yee and Zhao in 2022 [ 40 ] . We can easily notice that the above three classical theta series all arise from the Jacobi triple product series. Actually, Andrews–Merca [ 3 ] and Guo–Zeng [ 20 ] proposed the following conjecture for truncated Jacobi triple product series, independently. Conjecture 1.1 . For positive integers k , R , S k,R,S with 1 ≤ S < R / 2 1\leq S<R/2 , the coefficient of q n q^{n} with n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 in ( − 1 ) k − 1 ​ ∑ j = 0 k − 1 ( − 1 ) j ​ q R ​ j ​ ( j + 1 ) / 2 − S ​ j ​ ( 1 − q ( 2 ​ j + 1 ) ​ S ) ( q S , q R − S , q R , q R ) ∞ \displaystyle(-1)^{k-1}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(-1)^{j}q^{Rj(j+1)/2-Sj}\left(1-q^{(2j+1)S}\right)}{\left(q^{S},q^{R-S},q^{R};q^{R}\right)_{\infty}} (1.2) is nonnegative. The conjecture was proved by Mao [ 31 ] analytically and by Yee [ 42 ] combinatorially in 2015. By applying the theory of Bailey pairs and Liu’s expansion formula for q q -series, Wang and Yee [ 39 ] reproved this conjecture [ 29 ] . Recently, by utilizing the iteration of Bailey’s methods, the authors [ 16 ] obtained a general expression of ( 1.2 ) in the form of multiple sums and the results can reduce to Andrews-Gordon and Bressoud type identities. In 2021, Merca [ 32 , 33 ] proposed a stronger version of Conjecture 1.1 as follows. Conjecture 1.2 . For positive integers 1 ≤ S < R 1\leq S<R with k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , the theta series ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = k ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q R ​ j ​ ( j + 1 ) / 2 ​ ( q − S ​ j − q ( j + 1 ) ​ S ) ( q S , q R − S , q R ) ∞ \displaystyle(-1)^{k}\frac{\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{Rj(j+1)/2}\left(q^{-Sj}-q^{(j+1)S}\right)}{\left(q^{S},q^{R-S};q^{R}\right)_{\infty}} (1.3) has nonnegative coefficients. Ballantine and Feigon [ 7 ] proved the cases for k ∈ { 1 , 2 , 3 } k\in\{1,2,3\} of the above conjecture combinatorially. The authors confirmed the cases when R = 3 ​ S R=3S for S ≥ 1 S\geq 1 in [ 16 ] by proving the positivity of the coefficients of the following series ∑ j = 0 ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q ( 3 ​ j 2 + 6 ​ j ​ k + j ) / 2 ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ j + 2 ​ k + 1 ) ( q , q 2 , q 3 ) 2 . \displaystyle\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{\left(3j^{2}+6jk+j\right)/2}\left(1-q^{2j+2k+1}\right)}{(q,q^{2};q^{3})_{2}}. As a corollary, Merca’s another conjecture [ 8 , Conjecture 2] on 6 6 -regular partition was confirmed, where the ℓ \ell -regular partitions are the partitions with no part is divisible by ℓ \ell . This conjecture has been also confirmed by Yao [ 41 ] and Ballantine and Feigon verified a weaker form in [ 7 ] . Then, Zhou [ 43 ] proved the nonnegativity of the coefficient of the following series for seven cases of coprime α , β , γ \alpha,\beta,\gamma , ∑ j = 0 ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q ( 3 ​ j 2 + 6 ​ j ​ k + j ) / 2 ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ j + 2 ​ k + 1 ) ( 1 − q α ) ​ ( 1 − q β ) ​ ( 1 − q γ ) . \displaystyle\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{\left(3j^{2}+6jk+j\right)/2}\left(1-q^{2j+2k+1}\right)}{(1-q^{\alpha})(1-q^{\beta})(1-q^{\gamma})}. He also proposed a conjecture on the nonnegativity for the coefficient of a truncated two variable theta series. Notice that S S and R − S R-S are symmetric in our method and q ( R , S ) q^{(R,S)} can be replaced by q q , we can assume that 1 ≤ S < R / 2 1\leq S<R/2 and R , S R,\ S are coprime without losing of generality. In addition, the case of R = 2 ​ S R=2S is more simpler and will be proved as Corollary 4.4 in Section 4 . Then Conjecture 1.2 can be restated equivalently as follows. Conjecture 1.3 . For positive integers 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 , ( r , s ) = 1 (r,s)=1 and k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , the coefficient of q n q^{n} in ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = k ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q r ​ j ​ ( j + 1 ) / 2 ​ ( q − s ​ j − q ( j + 1 ) ​ s ) ( q s , q r − s , q r ) ∞ \displaystyle(-1)^{k}\frac{\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{rj(j+1)/2}\left(q^{-sj}-q^{(j+1)s}\right)}{\left(q^{s},q^{r-s};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}} (1.4) is nonnegative. In this paper, we are mainly devoted to determine the lower bound N N for n n such that for any given s , r s,r and k k , Merca’s conjecture holds for n ≥ N n\geq N , which is stated as follows. Theorem 1.4 . For positive coprime integers s s and r r with 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 and k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , there exists a constant N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) N(r,s,k) relative to r , s r,s and k k , such that when n ≥ N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) n\geq N(r,s,k) , the coefficient of q n q^{n} in ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = k ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q r ​ j ​ ( j + 1 ) / 2 ​ ( q − s ​ j − q ( j + 1 ) ​ s ) ( q s , q r − s , q r ) ∞ \displaystyle(-1)^{k}\frac{\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{rj(j+1)/2}\left(q^{-sj}-q^{(j+1)s}\right)}{\left(q^{s},q^{r-s};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}} is nonnegative. Actually, we not only certify the existence of such constant N N , but also provide a systematic method to calculate it precisely for given s , r s,r and k k . To do so, we decompose the denominator of the above theta series into two parts as follows, 1 ( q s , q r − s , q r ) ∞ = 1 ( q s , q r − s , q r ) 2 ⋅ 1 ( q 2 ​ r + s , q 3 ​ r − s , q r ) ∞ , \displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(q^{s},q^{r-s};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}}=\frac{1}{\left(q^{s},q^{r-s};q^{r}\right)_{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{\left(q^{2r+s},q^{3r-s};q^{r}\right)_{\infty}}, (1.5) where the first part has finite terms and the denominator of the second part is a nonmodular infinite product. Firstly, by using the residue theorem ( 2.6 ) and the involutions between partitions, we obtain the following theorem. Theorem 1.5 . For positive integers 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 , ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) = 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)=1 and k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , the coefficient of q n q^{n} in ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = 0 ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q r ⁡ ( j 2 + ( 2 ​ k + 1 ) ​ j ) / 2 − s ​ j ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ j ​ s + ( 2 ​ k + 1 ) ​ s ) ( 1 − q s ) ​ ( 1 − q r − s ) ​ ( 1 − q r + s ) ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ r − s ) , \displaystyle(-1)^{k}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{r(j^{2}+(2k+1)j)/2-sj}(1-q^{2js+(2k+1)s})}{(1-q^{s})(1-q^{r-s})(1-q^{r+s})(1-q^{2r-s})}, (1.6) is positive when n ≥ ( 2 ​ r 2 ​ s / k 3 + k ) ​ ( 4 ​ r 3 ​ s / k 3 − r + 2 ​ s ) . \displaystyle n\geq\left(2r^{2}\sqrt[3]{{s}/{k}}+k\right)\left(4r^{3}\sqrt[3]{{s}/{k}}-r+2s\right). (1.7) Consequently, when k k is large enough to make the right hand side of ( 1.7 ) negative, that is to say ( 1.6 ) has nonnegative coefficient for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , which directly deduce to Conjecture 1.3 holds for any s s and r r . Corollary 1.6 . When ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) = 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)=1 , 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 and k ≥ 64 ​ r 9 ​ s ( r − 2 ​ s ) 3 , \displaystyle k\geq\frac{64r^{9}s}{(r-2s)^{3}}, the theta series ( 1.6 ) has nonnegative coefficients for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , and thereby Conjecture 1.3 holds with the corresponding k k . When ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) ≠ 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)\neq 1 , we obtain the following two results. Theorem 1.7 . If 2 | s 2\mid s , 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 and k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , then the coefficient of q n q^{n} in ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = 0 ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q r ⁡ ( j 2 + ( 2 ​ k + 1 ) ​ j ) / 2 − s ​ j ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ j ​ s + ( 2 ​ k + 1 ) ​ s ) ( 1 − q s ) ​ ( 1 − q r − s ) ​ ( 1 − q r + s ) \displaystyle(-1)^{k}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{r(j^{2}+(2k+1)j)/2-sj}(1-q^{2js+(2k+1)s})}{(1-q^{s})(1-q^{r-s})(1-q^{r+s})} is positive if n ≥ ( p + k ) ​ ( 2 ​ p ​ r − r + 2 ​ s ) n\geq(p+k)(2pr-r+2s) , where p p is given in Section 4 and 6 , accurately and roughly, respectively. Theorem 1.8 . If 2 ∤ s 2\nmid s , 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 and k ≥ 1 k\geq 1 , then the coefficient of q n q^{n} in ( − 1 ) k ​ ∑ j = 0 ∞ ( − 1 ) j ​ q r ⁡ ( j 2 + ( 2 ​ k + 1 ) ​ j ) / 2 − s ​ j ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ j ​ s + ( 2 ​ k + 1 ) ​ s ) ( 1 − q s ) ​ ( 1 − q r − s ) ​ ( 1 − q 2 ​ r − s ) \displaystyle(-1)^{k}\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{j}q^{r(j^{2}+(2k+1)j)/2-sj}(1-q^{2js+(2k+1)s})}{(1-q^{s})(1-q^{r-s})(1-q^{2r-s})} is positive if n ≥ ( p + k ) ​ ( 2 ​ p ​ r / 15 − r + 2 ​ s ) n\geq(p+k)(2pr/15-r+2s) , where p p is given in Section 4 and 6 , accurately and roughly, respectively. Theorem 1.7 and Theorem 1.8 also have a version of Corollary 1.6 as follows, see the details in Section 6 . Corollary 1.9 . When 2 | s 2\mid s , 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 and k > 2 ​ r 3 ​ s − 2 ​ s ​ ( r − 2 ​ s ) r ⁡ ( r − 2 ​ s ) , \displaystyle k>\frac{2r^{3}s-2s(r-2s)}{r(r-2s)}, the theta series in Theorem 1.7 have nonnegative coefficients for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 . When 2 ∤ s 2\nmid s , 1 ≤ s < r / 2 1\leq s<r/2 and k > 4 ​ r 3 ​ s − 2 ​ s ​ ( r − 2 ​ s ) r ⁡ ( r − 2 ​ s ) , \displaystyle k>\frac{4r^{3}s-2s(r-2s)}{r(r-2s)}, the theta series in Theorem 1.8 have nonnegative coefficients for n ≥ 0 n\geq 0 , thereby Conjecture 1.3 holds for such two corresponding k k . Secondly, we observe that the second part on the right hand side of ( 1.5 ) is a nonmodular infinite product. The work on the asymptotic properties of nonmodular infinite products was first utilized by Lehner [ 28 ] and Livingood [ 30 ] and developed by Grosswald [ 19 ] . Recently, Chern used this method to prove Seo and Yee’s conjecture related to the index of seaweed algebras and partitions for larger n n , which was totally confirmed by Craig in [ 15 ] . With the idea, we can prove Conjecture 1.4 analytically for sufficient large n n . For positive integers 1 ≤ a < M / 2 1\leq a<M/2 and ( a , M ) = 1 (a,M)=1 , denote G ⁡ ( q ) = 1 ( q 2 ​ M + a , q 3 ​ M − a , q M ) ∞ = ∑ n = 0 ∞ g a , M ​ ( n ) ​ q \displaystyle G(q)=\frac{1}{(q^{2M+a},q^{3M-a};q^{M})_{\infty}}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}g_{a,M}(n)q (1.8) and Φ a , M = log ​ ( 1 ( q a , q M ) ∞ ) , Ψ a , M = log ​ ( 1 ( q 2 ​ M + a , q M ) ∞ ) . \displaystyle\Phi_{a,M}=\text{log}\left(\frac{1}{(q^{a};q^{M})_{\infty}}\right),\quad\Psi_{a,M}=\text{log}\left(\frac{1}{(q^{2M+a};q^{M})_{\infty}}\right). (1.9) In 1959, Iseki gave an asymptotic formula for the coefficient of G ⁡ ( q ) / ( q a , q M − a , q M ) 2 {G(q)}/{(q^{a},q^{M-a};q^{M})_{2}} as follows, see [ 25 ] . Chern also generalised the result in [ 11 ] . Theorem 1.10 (Iseki) . Let 1 ( q a , q M − a , q M ) ∞ = ∑ n − 0 ∞ g 1 ​ ( n ) ​ q n . \displaystyle\frac{1}{(q^{a},q^{M-a};q^{M})_{\infty}}=\sum_{n-0}^{\infty}g_{1}(n)q^{n}. When n n is sufficient large, g 1 ​ ( n ) ∼ 1 4 ​ sin ⁡ a ​ π M ​ ( 3 ​ M ) − 1 4 ​ n − 3 4 ​ exp ​ ( 2 ​ π ​ n 3 ​ M ) . \displaystyle g_{1}(n)\sim\frac{1}{4\sin\frac{a\pi}{M}}\left(3M\right)^{-\frac{1}{4}}n^{-\frac{3}{4}}\mathrm{exp}\left(2\pi\sqrt{\frac{n}{3M}}\right). By using the circle method, we further extend Chern’s idea to derive the following expression for the nonmodular infinite product Ψ a , M ​ ( q ) + Ψ M − a , M ​ ( q ) \Psi_{a,M}(q)+\Psi_{M-a,M}(q) . Theorem 1.11 . Let X ≥ 16 X\geq 16 be a sufficiently large positive number. Let q := e − τ + 2 ​ π ​ i ​ h k 1 , q:=e^{-\tau+\frac{2\pi ih}{k_{1}}}, where 1 ≤ h ≤ k 1 ≤ ⌊ 2 ​ π ​ X ⌋ = : N 1\leq h\leq k_{1}\leq\lfloor\sqrt{2\pi X}\rfloor=:N with ( h , k 1 ) = 1 (h,k_{1})=1 and τ := X − 1 + 2 ​ π ​ i ​ Y \tau:=X^{-1}+2\pi iY with | Y | ≤ 1 k 1 ​ N |Y|\leq\frac{1}{k_{1}N} . We obtain an asymptotic formula for the coefficient of G ⁡ ( q ) G(q) , for which the details will be given in Section 6 . Theorem 1.12 . When n n is sufficient large, g a , M ​ ( n ) ∼ 1 2 ​ Δ M ​ sin ⁡ a ​ π M ​ ( π 2 3 ​ M ​ n ) 5 2 ​ I − 5 ​ ( 2 ​ π ​ n 3 ​ M ) . \displaystyle g_{a,M}(n)\sim\frac{1}{2\Delta_{M}\sin\frac{a\pi}{M}}\left({\frac{\pi^{2}}{3Mn}}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}I_{-5}\left(2\pi\sqrt{\frac{n}{3M}}\right). where I v ​ ( n ) I_{v}(n) is the modified Bessel function of the ﬁrst kind and Δ M := a ⁡ ( M − a ) ​ ( M + a ) ​ ( 2 ​ M − a ) \Delta_{M}:=a(M-a)(M+a)(2M-a) . By replacing a , M a,M by s , r s,r in ( 1.8 ), respectively, we also have the bounds for g s , r ​ ( n ) g_{s,r}(n) . Theorem 1.13 . when n n is sufficient large, g s , r ​ ( n ) ≥ 0.99 ​ π 4 2 ​ ( 3 ​ r ) 11 4 ​ Δ ​ sin ⁡ ( s ​ π / r ) ​ n − 11 4 ​ exp ​ ( 2 ​ π ​ n 3 ​ r ) = : g d ​ ( n ) , \displaystyle g_{s,r}(n)\geq\frac{0.99\pi^{4}}{2(3r)^{\frac{11}{4}}\Delta\sin(s\pi/r)}n^{-\frac{11}{4}}\mathrm{exp}\left(2\pi\sqrt{\frac{n}{3r}}\right)=:g^{d}(n), g s , r ​ ( n ) ≤ 1.01 ​ π 4 2 ​ ( 3 ​ r ) 11 4 ​ Δ ​ sin ⁡ ( s ​ π / r ) ​ n − 11 4 ​ exp ​ ( 2 ​ π ​ n 3 ​ r ) = : g u ​ ( n ) , \displaystyle g_{s,r}(n)\leq\frac{1.01\pi^{4}}{2(3r)^{\frac{11}{4}}\Delta\sin(s\pi/r)}n^{-\frac{11}{4}}\mathrm{exp}\left(2\pi\sqrt{\frac{n}{3r}}\right)=:g^{u}(n), where Δ := s ⁡ ( r − s ) ​ ( r + s ) ​ ( 2 ​ r − s ) . \Delta:=s(r-s)(r+s)(2r-s). Above all, by combining the results as given in Theorem 1.5 (or Theorem 1.7 and 1.8 respectively) and Theorem 1.13 we can prove Theorem 1.4 , which confirms Conjecture 1.3 for large n n . This paper is organized as follows. In Section 2 , we shall introduce some basic definitions and notations on q q -series and Bessel integrals. In Section 3 and Section 4 , we aim to prove Theorems 1.5 - 1.8 with coprime and non coprime parameters, respectively. In Section 5 , we discuss the nonmodular infinite product, together with bounds on minor arcs of G ⁡ ( q ) G(q) and its approximations near the poles. Section 6 is mainly concerned with the derivation of the asymptotic formula for g a , M ​ ( n ) g_{a,M}(n) by using the circle method and the proof of Theorem 1.4 . In Section 7 , we give some concluding remarks based on our method. The appendix lists some verbose constant terms omitted in Section 3 and 4 . 2 Preliminaries Throughout this paper, we adopt standard notations and terminologies for q q -series, see, for example, [ 18 ] . We assume that | q | < 1 \absolutevalue{q}<1 . The q q -shifted factorial is denoted by ( a ) n = ( a , q ) n = { 1 , if n = 0 , ( 1 − a ) ( 1 − a q ) ⋯ ( 1 − a q n − 1 ) , if n ≥ 1 . (a)_{n}=(a;q)_{n}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{\it if $n=0$},\\[5.69054pt] (1-a)(1-aq)\cdots(1-aq^{n-1}),&\text{\it if $n\geq 1$}.\end{cases} We also use the notation ( a ) ∞ = ( a , q ) ∞ = ∏ n = 0 ∞ ( 1 − a ​ q n ) . (a)_{\infty}=(a;q)_{\infty}=\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}(1-aq^{n}). There are more compact notations for the multiple q q -shifted factorials ( a 1 , a 2 , … , a m , q ) n \displaystyle(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m};q)_{n} = ( a 1 ; q ) n ( a 2 ; q ) n ⋯ ( a m ; q ) n , \displaystyle=(a_{1};q)_{n}(a_{2};q)_{n}\cdots(a_{m};q)_{n}, ( a 1 , a 2 , … , a m , q ) ∞ \displaystyle(a_{1},a_{2},\dots,a_{m};q)_{\infty} = ( a 1 ; q ) ∞ ( a 2 ; q ) ∞ ⋯ ( a m ; q ) ∞ . \displaystyle=(a_{1};q)_{\infty}(a_{2};q)_{\infty}\cdots(a_{m};q)_{\infty}. The well-known Jacobi triple product identity is given by [ 18 , II.28] ∑ k = − ∞ ∞ ( − 1 ) k ​ q ( k 2 ) ​ a k = ( a , q / a , q , q ) ∞ . \sum_{k=-\infty}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}q^{k\choose 2}a^{k}=(a,q/a,q;q)_{\infty}. (2.1) Truncated theta series is closely related to integer partitions. Recall that a partition of a nonnegative integer n n is a finite nonincreasing sequence of positive integers λ 1 , λ 2 , … , λ k \lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\ldots,\lambda_{k} such that ∑ i = 1 k λ i = n \sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_{i}=n , see, for example, [ 2 ] . Let p ⁡ ( n ) p(n) denote the number of integer partitions of n n with p ⁡ ( 0 ) = 1 p(0)=1 , which has the generating function ∑ n = 0 ∞ p ⁡ ( n ) ​ q n = 1 ( q , q ) ∞ . \sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p(n)q^{n}=\frac{1}{(q;q)_{\infty}}. We then reveal the following important result given by Polya and Szegö. Lemma 2.1 ( [ 35 , Problem 27.1] ) . Let a 1 , a 2 , … , a ℓ a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{\ell} be positive integers with ( a i , a j ) = 1 (a_{i},a_{j})=1 when i ≠ j i\neq j , then 1 ( 1 − q a 1 ) ( 1 − q a 2 ) ⋯ ( 1 − q a ℓ ) = R ⁡ ( 1 1 − q ) + S ⁡ ( q ) 1 − q a 1 a 2 ⋯ a ℓ , \displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-q^{a_{1}})(1-q^{a_{2}})\cdots(1-q^{a_{\ell}})}=R\Big(\frac{1}{1-q}\Big)+\frac{S(q)}{1-q^{a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{\ell}}}, (2.2) where R ⁡ ( x ) R(x) and S ⁡ ( x ) S(x) are polynomials of x x with degree ℓ − 1 \ell-1 and smaller than a 1 a 2 ⋯ a ℓ a_{1}a_{2}\cdots a_{\ell} , respectively. Let p ℓ ​ ( n ) p_{\ell}(n) be the number of partitions with coprime numbers a 1 , a 2 , … , a ℓ a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{\ell} as parts, then the generating function of p ℓ ​ ( n ) p_{\ell}(n) can be stated as follows ∑ n = 0 ∞ p ℓ ​ ( n ) = 1 ( 1 − q a 1 ) ( 1 − q a 2 ) ⋯ ( 1 − q a ℓ ) . \displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p_{\ell}(n)=\frac{1}{(1-q^{a_{1}})(1-q^{a_{2}})\cdots(1-q^{a_{\ell}})}. (2.3) By employing Lemma 2.1 with ℓ = 2 \ell=2 , it leads to the following result. Lemma 2.2 ( [ 35 , Problem 26] ) . We have p 2 ​ ( n ) = ⌊ n a 1 ​ a 2 ⌋ ​ or ​ p 2 ​ ( n ) = ⌊ n a 1 ​ a 2 ⌋ + 1 , p_{2}(n)=\left\lfloor\frac{n}{a_{1}a_{2}}\right\rfloor\text{ or }\ p_{2}(n)=\left\lfloor\frac{n}{a_{1}a_{2}}\right\rfloor+1, when ⌊ a ⌋ \lfloor a\rfloor denotes the greatest integer no more than a . a. Next, we recall some basic notation and properties which will be applied in this paper. The symbol ( a 1 , a 2 , … , a ℓ ) (a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{\ell}) is used to denote the greatest common divisor of a 1 , a 2 , … , a ℓ a_{1},a_{2},\ldots,a_{\ell} . Let n ≡ a n\equiv a denote the remainder of n n modulo a a . For example, if n = 25 n=25 , a = 7 a=7 , then 25 ≡ 7 = 4 25\equiv 7=4 . From this formula, we obtain the explicit bound for G ⁡ ( q ) G(q) on the minor arcs and near the poles. 5.1 Proof of Theorem 1.11 In this subsection, we prove the expression for Ψ a , M ​ ( q ) + Ψ M − a , M ​ ( q ) \Psi_{a,M}(q)+\Psi_{M-a,M}(q) as given in Theorem 1.11 . For brevity, we omit the details for Case 3 which is just similar with Case 2, then the proof is complete. 6 The asymptotic formula and Theorem 1.4 The circle method was first used by Hardy and Ramanujan [ 21 ] in 1918 to investigate the asymptotic growth of the partition function p ⁡ ( n ) p(n) , which was later refined to deal with the coefficients of the q q -expansions of modular forms [ 36 ] . Recently, Chern [ 12 ] extended this method to study the nonmodular infinite products. The key idea of this method is to write the function under consideration into an integral over a circle, then transforming the integral into something more tractable to derive the asymptotic formula for the coefficient of the function. To be more precisely, throughout this section, we assume that X = 3 ​ M ​ n π 2 . \displaystyle X=\sqrt{\frac{3Mn}{\pi^{2}}}. From Lemma 5.1 , we set X ≥ 540 ​ M 5 X\geq 540M^{5} , and thereby the bound for n n is given as follows n ≥ ( 4.63 ​ M ) 9 . \displaystyle n\geq(4.63M)^{9}. (6.1) 6.1 Proof of Theorem 1.12 In this section, we will employ the circle method to estimate g a , M ​ ( n ) g_{a,M}(n) for sufficiently large n n . Based on Theorem 5.1 and Theorem 5.2 , we are ready to prove Theorem 1.12 . Proof of Theorem 1.12 . In summary, we state the systematic method to prove Merca’s conjecture 1.3 completely or determine the constant N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) N(r,s,k) for given r , s r,s and k k such that when n ≥ N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) n\geq N(r,s,k) the conjecture holds. Algorithm for the proof of Merca’s stronger conjecture 1.3 : Case 1: If ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) = 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)=1 , N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) N(r,s,k) can be determined as follows: Step 1.1: Compute N 1 ​ ( r , s , k ) = ( 2 ​ r 2 ​ s / k 3 + k ) ​ ( 4 ​ r 3 ​ s / k 3 − r + 2 ​ s ) N_{1}(r,s,k)=\left(2r^{2}\sqrt[3]{s/k}+k\right)\left(4r^{3}\sqrt[3]{s/k}-r+2s\right) as given in ( 3.23 ). Using computer programs, if we can show that the first N 1 ​ ( r , s , k ) N_{1}(r,s,k) terms of the theta series ( 3.13 ) have nonnegative coefficients, then Conjecture 1.3 holds directly as a corollary. Alternatively, if k k is large enough and satisfies the condition in Corollary 1.6 , Merca’s conjecture holds directly, too. Otherwise, go to Step 1.2. Step 1.2: By computing the convolution of the series for C ⁡ ( n ) C(n) and g s , r ​ ( n ) g_{s,r}(n) as shown in ( 6.20 ), it follows that N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) = max ​ { 4 ​ F 1 2 , 2 ⋅ ( 4.63 ​ r ) 9 } + F 1 + r ​ k ​ ( k + 1 ) / 2 − s ​ k , N(r,s,k)=\text{max}\left\{4F^{2}_{1},2\cdot(4.63r)^{9}\right\}+F_{1}+rk(k+1)/2-sk, where F 1 F_{1} and p p are given in ( 6.17 ) and ( 6.18 ). Case 2: If ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) ≠ 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)\neq 1 and 2 | s 2\mid s , the procedures are as follows: Step 2.1: For given r , s , k r,s,k , let z 0 z_{0} be the maximum zero of D 0 , d ​ ( t 1 , p , p ) D_{0,d}(t_{1,p},p) , D 1 , d ​ ( t 1 , p , p ) D_{1,d}(t_{1,p},p) , D 1 , d ​ ( − r + p ​ r + 2 ​ k ​ p ​ r + 2 ​ p 2 ​ r − s / 2 , p ) D_{1,d}(-r+pr+2kpr+2p^{2}r-s/2,p) , D 2 , d ​ ( t 2 , p , p ) D_{2,d}(t_{2,p},p) , D 3 , d ​ ( t 3 , p , p ) D_{3,d}(t_{3,p},p) , D 3 , d ​ ( t 4 , p , p ) D_{3,d}(t_{4,p},p) , which are polynomials relative to 4 ​ p 4p of degree 2 2 . Compute N 2 ​ ( r , s , k ) = ( z 0 + k ) ​ ( 2 ​ z 0 ​ r − r + 2 ​ s ) . N_{2}(r,s,k)=(z_{0}+k)(2z_{0}r-r+2s). Moreover, if we can show the theta series ( 4.5 ) has nonnegative terms for 0 ≤ n ≤ N 2 ​ ( r , s , k ) 0\leq n\leq N_{2}(r,s,k) , then Conjecture 1.3 holds directly as a corollary. Else if k k satisfies the condition in Corollary 1.9 , Merca’s conjecture holds immediately for such k k . Otherwise, go to Step 2.2. Step 2.2: By studying the convolution of the series for J s , r ​ ( n ) J_{s,r}(n) and D ⁡ ( n ) D(n) as given in ( 6.31 ), we have N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) = max ​ { 4 ​ F 1 2 , 2 ⋅ ( 4.63 ​ r ) 9 } + F 1 + r ​ k ​ ( k + 1 ) / 2 − s ​ k , \displaystyle N(r,s,k)=\text{max}\left\{4F^{2}_{1},2\cdot(4.63r)^{9}\right\}+F_{1}+rk(k+1)/2-sk, where p ≥ r 4 p\geq r^{4} and F 1 = ( r 4 + k ) ​ ( 2 ⋅ r 4 ⋅ r − r + 2 ​ s ) F_{1}=(r^{4}+k)(2\cdot r^{4}\cdot r-r+2s) . Case 3: If ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) ≠ 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)\neq 1 , and 2 ∤ s 2\nmid s , the procedure is similar to Case 2 and we just need to consider E ⁡ ( n ) E(n) and J r − s , r ​ ( n ) J_{r-s,r}(n) . Step 3.1: For given r , s , k r,s,k , let z 0 z_{0} be the maximum zero of E 0 , d ​ ( t 1 , p , p ) E_{0,d}(t_{1,p},p) , E 1 , d ​ ( t 1 , p , p ) E_{1,d}(t_{1,p},p) , E 1 , d ​ ( − r + p ​ r + 2 ​ k ​ p ​ r + 2 ​ p 2 ​ r − s / 2 , p ) E_{1,d}(-r+pr+2kpr+2p^{2}r-s/2,p) , E 2 , d ​ ( t 2 , p , p ) E_{2,d}(t_{2,p},p) , E 3 , d ​ ( t 3 , p , p ) E_{3,d}(t_{3,p},p) , E 3 , d ​ ( t 4 , p , p ) E_{3,d}(t_{4,p},p) . Compute N 3 ​ ( r , s , k ) = ( z 0 + k ) ​ ( 2 ​ z 0 ​ r − r + 2 ​ s ) . N_{3}(r,s,k)=(z_{0}+k)(2z_{0}r-r+2s). Moreover, if we can show the theta series ( 4.5 ) has nonnegative terms for 0 ≤ n ≤ N 3 ​ ( r , s , k ) 0\leq n\leq N_{3}(r,s,k) or the k k satisfies the condition in Corollary 1.9 , then Conjecture 1.3 holds directly as a corollary. Step 3.2: By studying the convolution of the series for J r − s , r ​ ( n ) J_{r-s,r}(n) and E ⁡ ( n ) E(n) similar to ( 6.31 ), we have N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) = max ​ { 4 ​ F 1 2 , 2 ⋅ ( 4.63 ​ r ) 9 } + F 1 + r ​ k ​ ( k + 1 ) / 2 − s ​ k , \displaystyle N(r,s,k)=\text{max}\left\{4F^{2}_{1},2\cdot(4.63r)^{9}\right\}+F_{1}+rk(k+1)/2-sk, where p ≥ 64 ​ r 4 / 15 p\geq 64r^{4}/15 and F 1 = ( 64 ​ r 4 / 15 + k ) ​ ( 2 ⋅ 64 ​ r 4 / 15 ⋅ r − r + 2 ​ s ) F_{1}=(64r^{4}/15+k)(2\cdot 64r^{4}/15\cdot r-r+2s) . We also remark that for the bound N 1 ​ ( r , s , k ) N_{1}(r,s,k) in Case 1, We can also improve it by using similar method as given in Case 2 and Case 3, in which we need to consider the maximum roots for certain polynomials. Now, we give an example to illustrate the process to determine N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) N(r,s,k) when ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) = 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)=1 . Example 6.1 . When r = 12 , s = 1 , k = 1 r=12,s=1,k=1 , since ( s , r − s , r + s , 2 ​ r − s ) = 1 (s,r-s,r+s,2r-s)=1 , we can follow the above discussion to derive N ⁡ ( r , s , k ) N(r,s,k) . Then by dividing these ℓ \ell integers { i ​ r + s } 0 ≤ i < ℓ 1 \{ir+s\}_{0\leq i<\ell_{1}} and { ( j + 1 ) ​ r − s } 0 ≤ j < ℓ 2 \{(j+1)r-s\}_{0\leq j<\ell_{2}} into groups of coprime numbers and following the similar discussions based on Lemma 2.1 and the circle method, one can try to show the nonnegativity of the coefficients of q n q^{n} . Moreover, employing the same method, one may treat the following truncated quintuple product identity, which was investigate by Chan, Ho and Mao in [ 13 ] : ∑ j = − k k ( − 1 ) j ​ q R ​ j ​ ( 3 ​ j + 1 ) ​ ( q 3 ​ j ​ S − q − ( 3 ​ j + 1 ) ​ S ) ( q − S , q R + S , q R , q R ) ∞ ​ ( q R − 2 ​ S , q R + 2 ​ S , q 2 ​ R ) ∞ . \displaystyle\frac{\sum_{j=-k}^{k}(-1)^{j}q^{Rj(3j+1)}\left(q^{3jS}-q^{-(3j+1)S}\right)}{\left(q^{-S},q^{R+S},q^{R};q^{R}\right)_{\infty}\left(q^{R-2S},q^{R+2S};q^{2R}\right)_{\infty}}. At last, we proposed the following generalized truncated version for Conjecture 1.1 . Conjecture 7.1 . For positive integers 1 ≤ S < R 1\leq S<R with k > ℓ ≥ 1 k>\ell\geq 1 , k ≥ 4 k\geq 4 , there exists a sufficient large integer N N such that the theta series ( − 1 ) k − 1 ( ∑ j = − k + 1 k − ∑ j = − ℓ + 1 ℓ ) ( − 1 ) j q R ​ j ​ ( j + 1 ) / 2 + S ​ j ( q S , q R − S , q R , q R ) ∞ \displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k-1}\left(\sum_{j=-k+1}^{k}-\sum_{j=-\ell+1}^{\ell}\right)(-1)^{j}q^{Rj(j+1)/2+Sj}}{\left(q^{S},q^{R-S},q^{R};q^{R}\right)_{\infty}} (7.2) has nonnegative coefficients when n ≥ N n\geq N . The above conjecture is equivalent to the following inequality for the partition function P R , S ​ ( n ) P_{R,S}(n) , where P R , S ​ ( n ) P_{R,S}(n) denotes the number of partition of n n with any part λ i ≡ ± S , R ( mod R ) . \lambda_{i}\equiv\pm S,R\ (\bmod\ R). Conjecture 7.2 . Under the same conditions as given in Conjecture 7.1 , we have that for n ≥ N n\geq N ( − 1 ) k − 1 ( ∑ j = − k + 1 − ℓ + ∑ j = ℓ + 1 k ) ( − 1 ) j P R , S ( n − ( R j ( j + 1 ) / 2 + S j ) ) ≥ 0 . \displaystyle(-1)^{k-1}\left(\sum_{j=-k+1}^{-\ell}+\sum_{j=\ell+1}^{k}\right)(-1)^{j}P_{R,S}\Big(n-\left(Rj(j+1)/2+Sj\right)\Big)\geq 0. For Conjecture 7.1 , if k − ℓ ≡ 1 ( mod 2 ) k-\ell\equiv 1\ (\bmod\ 2) , then N = 0 N=0 . Thus the conjecture is a direct corollary of Conjecture 1.1 and we have the following theorem. Theorem 7.3 . When k − ℓ ≡ 1 ( mod 2 ) k-\ell\equiv 1\ (\bmod\ 2) , we have ( − 1 ) ℓ ( ∑ j = − k + 1 − ℓ + 1 + ∑ j = ℓ k ) ( − 1 ) j P R , S ( n − ( R j ( j + 1 ) / 2 + S j ) ) ≥ 0 . \displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\left(\sum_{j=-k+1}^{-\ell+1}+\sum_{j=\ell}^{k}\right)(-1)^{j}P_{R,S}(n-\left(Rj(j+1)/2+Sj\right))\geq 0. Instructions for reporting errors We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of the methods listed below: Click the "Report Issue" ( ) button, located in the page header. Tip: You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your report. Our team has already identified the following issues . We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for all. Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a list of packages that need conversion , and welcome developer contributions .
